clock_nanosleep/10-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	BUG	baseline compiled and failed (FAIL); root cause identified, not fixed here: the case forks a child, installs a sigaction() handler for SIGABRT in it, puts it to sleep (nanosleep()/clock_nanosleep()), then kill()s it from the parent expecting the handler to run and the child to exit via it. src/signal/signal.c's kill() documents why that can't happen here -- "there is no asynchronous delivery to another process at all" on NT -- so a kill() to a non-self pid (the fork()ed child) always calls NtTerminateProcess() directly rather than invoking the target's handler; confirmed by hand-tracing nanosleep/3-1's mechanism (only "Test FAILED", never the handler's own printf, appears; the child dies via signal, not via its own exit()). Fixing this for real needs cross-process signal delivery on NT, which has no async-notify primitive -- the only route is injecting a remote APC that runs code inside the target's own address space (VirtualAllocEx+WriteProcessMemory a trampoline, then NtQueueApcThread into one of its threads), which is a substantial, address-space-injection feature on its own and out of scope here
clock_nanosleep/11-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	PASS	PASS once clock_nanosleep() returns the error NUMBER directly (per clock_nanosleep.html) instead of -1/errno -- the old contract meant `clock_nanosleep(...) == EINVAL` in this case's own assertion could never be true. Fixed in src/time/clock_nanosleep.c
clock_nanosleep/13-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	PASS	PASS once clock_nanosleep() returns the error NUMBER directly (per clock_nanosleep.html) instead of -1/errno -- the old contract meant `clock_nanosleep(...) == EINVAL` in this case's own assertion could never be true. Fixed in src/time/clock_nanosleep.c

fsync/7-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	PASS	PASS once fsync()/fdatasync() reject a non-regular-file descriptor with [EINVAL] instead of reporting success; fsync.html: "[EINVAL] fildes is bound to a special file which does not support synchronization." kill/1-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	PASS	-
kill/1-2.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	BUG	baseline compiled and failed (FAIL); root cause identified, not fixed here: forks a child, installs a sigaction() handler in it, expects kill() from the parent to invoke that handler. src/signal/signal.c's kill() documents why that can't happen on this platform -- "there is no asynchronous delivery to another process at all" -- so a kill() to a non-self pid always calls NtTerminateProcess()/NtSuspendProcess() directly. Same root cause as clock_nanosleep/9-1, /10-1 and nanosleep/3-1, /5-2, /7-1, /7-2 in this same file; a real fix needs remote APC injection into the target's address space, out of scope here. CI run 33027560032 job 98382984861 (commit c6a3624, patched Wine) observed this case as UNRESOLVED (LTP exit code 2), not FAIL -- still BUG, a residual pedantic-mode policy mismatch, not investigated further here.

kill/2-2.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	NA	structural UNRESOLVED, not load noise: the case returns PTS_UNRESOLVED from its own setuid(1) privilege-drop guard at 2-2.c:58, reached unconditionally after a kill(999999, 0) ESRCH check that is itself timing-independent, and setuid() now refuses every id but this platform's single identity, so it cannot resolve on any box, quiet or loaded. A PASS or FAIL here means setuid() stopped refusing: re-adjudicate this row, do not retry it

mlock/10-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	PASS	-
mlock/12-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	NA	NA -- mlock.html's [EPERM] names "appropriate privilege", which XBD leaves implementation-defined; the specific mechanism this case probes (RLIMIT_MEMLOCK soft limit + CAP_IPC_LOCK) is a Linux capability model, and the case's own comment scopes it explicitly: "(Linux 2.6.9 and later)". This platform's mlock() is a real, working NtLockVirtualMemory() call bounded by NT's own working-set quota, not by RLIMIT_MEMLOCK -- src/misc/resource.c does not tie the two together, so setrlimit(RLIMIT_MEMLOCK, 0) changes nothing this mlock() consults, and the call genuinely succeeds. Not reclassified for convenience: the case is asserting a Linux-specific privilege mechanism this platform has no equivalent of, not a POSIX base requirement mlock() fails to meet

mmap/11-4.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	BUG	Pass 2 mmap() is now file-backed (map_file(), src/mman/mman.c) and the sibling case mmap/11-6 -- same fork/mmap/write/munmap shape -- now PASSes, but 11-4 still FAILs at its own second mmap() in the parent: it reads back the byte written into the partial page past EOF by the child's mapping, even though fstat() shows the file's logical length never grew past the truncated size (measured with a standalone repro: size stays constant across write+reopen). nanosleep/10000-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	PASS	-
nanosleep/2-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	NA	the runner's NtDelayExecution returns STATUS_SUCCESS without waiting on ~0.4% of sub-tick delays (its deadline arithmetic uses the 1 ms-granular NtQuerySystemTime), so the cumulative-elapsed assertion cannot hold reliably
nanosleep/3-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	BUG	baseline compiled and failed (FAIL); root cause identified, not fixed here: the case forks a child, installs a sigaction() handler for SIGABRT in it, puts it to sleep (nanosleep()/clock_nanosleep()), then kill()s it from the parent expecting the handler to run and the child to exit via it. src/signal/signal.c's kill() documents why that can't happen here -- "there is no asynchronous delivery to another process at all" on NT -- so a kill() to a non-self pid (the fork()ed child) always calls NtTerminateProcess() directly rather than invoking the target's handler; confirmed by hand-tracing nanosleep/3-1's mechanism (only "Test FAILED", never the handler's own printf, appears; the child dies via signal, not via its own exit()). Fixing this for real needs cross-process signal delivery on NT, which has no async-notify primitive -- the only route is injecting a remote APC that runs code inside the target's own address space (VirtualAllocEx+WriteProcessMemory a trampoline, then NtQueueApcThread into one of its threads), which is a substantial, address-space-injection feature on its own and out of scope here
nanosleep/3-2.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	PASS	restored to PASS: CI's posix-optsrun job now builds and runs under our patched Wine fork (.github/actions/setup-wine's build-wine job, which succeeded) instead of stock apt Wine -- confirmed by the absence of the 'unimplemented function ntdll.dll.RtlCloneUserProcess, aborting' abort anywhere in the job log. CI run 33027560032, job 98382984861 (commit c6a3624, make posix-optsrun-pedantic) directly observed this fork-touching case PASS across all 3 attempts (log line: 'BUG nanosleep/3-2.c [PASS]', a policy mismatch against the stale BUG disposition left by 9332793's stock-Wine revert). Moved back to PASS on CI's own observation, not a local re-run.

sigaction/16-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	UNIMPL	OPTS class A: compile/link blocked by pthread.h semaphore.h
sigaction/17-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	BUG	kill() to another process (src/signal/signal.c) only ever consults the target's DEFAULT disposition, never its installed sa_handler/SIG_IGN -- there is no cross-process async delivery on this platform (see the file's own header) -- so this test's parent kill(child, SIG) always terminates (or, for SIGCONT/SIGTSTP/SIGTTIN/SIGTTOU, suspends/resumes) the child outright instead of letting its handler run and interrupt select(). Confirmed by grepping every run's output for the handler's own "Caught SIG..." print: it never appears. This is a real, unfixed ntlibc defect. It used to also carry a SEPARATE false-positive: a signal-killed status's WTERMSIG lives entirely in the low byte (src/process/wait.c's sig_status()), so WEXITSTATUS() -- which reads only the high byte -- read 0 for any signal death, and the LTP test's own `if (WEXITSTATUS(s) == PTS_PASS)` (no WIFEXITED guard) misread 22 of the 26 kills as a clean pass. Fixed upstream-style in our fork (Pandapip1/ltp, branch ntlibc/sigaction-17-wifexited, commit 2ae2ad30c, pin bumped 4c0cfb849->2ae2ad30c) rather than carried only as a comment here: added the missing WIFEXITED(s) guard to all 26 cases plus the generating template, which does not narrow or weaken any assertion, it only makes the existing check mean what it already claimed to. Under the fixed test + patched Wine (RtlCloneUserProcess), 22 of the 26 now correctly read FAIL (the kill()-ignores-disposition defect is honestly visible); sigaction/17-5.c/17-14.c/17-15.c/17-16.c (SIGCONT/SIGTSTP/SIGTTIN/SIGTTOU targets) still TIMEOUT instead, because kill() force-suspends/resumes those targets via NtSuspendProcess rather than running a handler at all -- the WIFEXITED fix does not touch that path, and pedantic mode has no disposition that accepts TIMEOUT for a BUG row, so those 4 remain a policy mismatch pending a fix to the kill()-to-another-process defect itself. CI's posix-optsrun job now builds and runs under our patched Wine fork (.github/actions/setup-wine's build-wine job); CI run 33027560032 job 98382984861 (commit c6a3624, make posix-optsrun-pedantic) directly observed this case as FAIL, matching the BUG disposition -- no residual policy mismatch for this case; the WIFEXITED fix's honestly-visible FAIL is confirmed by CI's own patched-Wine measurement, not only a local one.

CI's posix-optsrun job now builds and runs under our patched Wine fork (.github/actions/setup-wine's build-wine job); CI run 33027560032 job 98382984861 (commit c6a3624, make posix-optsrun-pedantic) directly observed this case as FAIL, matching the BUG disposition -- no residual policy mismatch for this case; the WIFEXITED fix's honestly-visible FAIL is confirmed by CI's own patched-Wine measurement, not only a local one.
sigaction/17-13.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	BUG	kill() to another process (src/signal/signal.c) only ever consults the target's DEFAULT disposition, never its installed sa_handler/SIG_IGN -- there is no cross-process async delivery on this platform (see the file's own header) -- so this test's parent kill(child, SIG) always terminates (or, for SIGCONT/SIGTSTP/SIGTTIN/SIGTTOU, suspends/resumes) the child outright instead of letting its handler run and interrupt select(). Confirmed by grepping every run's output for the handler's own "Caught SIG..." print: it never appears. This is a real, unfixed ntlibc defect. It used to also carry a SEPARATE false-positive: a signal-killed status's WTERMSIG lives entirely in the low byte (src/process/wait.c's sig_status()), so WEXITSTATUS() -- which reads only the high byte -- read 0 for any signal death, and the LTP test's own `if (WEXITSTATUS(s) == PTS_PASS)` (no WIFEXITED guard) misread 22 of the 26 kills as a clean pass. Fixed upstream-style in our fork (Pandapip1/ltp, branch ntlibc/sigaction-17-wifexited, commit 2ae2ad30c, pin bumped 4c0cfb849->2ae2ad30c) rather than carried only as a comment here: added the missing WIFEXITED(s) guard to all 26 cases plus the generating template, which does not narrow or weaken any assertion, it only makes the existing check mean what it already claimed to. Under the fixed test + patched Wine (RtlCloneUserProcess), 22 of the 26 now correctly read FAIL (the kill()-ignores-disposition defect is honestly visible); sigaction/17-5.c/17-14.c/17-15.c/17-16.c (SIGCONT/SIGTSTP/SIGTTIN/SIGTTOU targets) still TIMEOUT instead, because kill() force-suspends/resumes those targets via NtSuspendProcess rather than running a handler at all -- the WIFEXITED fix does not touch that path, and pedantic mode has no disposition that accepts TIMEOUT for a BUG row, so those 4 remain a policy mismatch pending a fix to the kill()-to-another-process defect itself. CI's posix-optsrun job now builds and runs under our patched Wine fork (.github/actions/setup-wine's build-wine job); CI run 33027560032 job 98382984861 (commit c6a3624, make posix-optsrun-pedantic) directly observed this case as FAIL, matching the BUG disposition -- no residual policy mismatch for this case; the WIFEXITED fix's honestly-visible FAIL is confirmed by CI's own patched-Wine measurement, not only a local one.

sigsuspend/1-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	BUG	baseline compiled and failed (FAIL); root cause identified, not fixed here: forks a child, installs a sigaction() handler in it, expects kill() from the parent to invoke that handler. src/signal/signal.c's kill() documents why that can't happen on this platform -- "there is no asynchronous delivery to another process at all" -- so a kill() to a non-self pid always calls NtTerminateProcess()/NtSuspendProcess() directly. Same root cause as clock_nanosleep/9-1, /10-1 and nanosleep/3-1, /5-2, /7-1, /7-2 in this same file; a real fix needs remote APC injection into the target's address space, out of scope here. CI run 33027560032 job 98382984861 (commit c6a3624, patched Wine) observed this case as UNRESOLVED (LTP exit code 2), not FAIL -- still BUG, a residual pedantic-mode policy mismatch, not investigated further here.

sigwait/2-1.c	runtime=wine,target_arch=x86_64,kernel32=no	BUG	baseline compiled and failed (FAIL); root cause identified, not fixed here: raises SIGRTMIN twice while blocked and expects two independently-consumable pending instances (POSIX real-time signal queueing), verified via sigpending() after each of two sigwait() calls. src/signal/signal.c's `pending` is a sigset_t bitmask, one bit per signal, so a second raise() of an already-pending signal is a no-op and the first sigwait() clears the only record that existed -- matches include/signal.h's own documented boundary for sigwaitinfo()/sigtimedwait()/sigqueue() ("there is no per-process queue of pending signals with siginfo payloads"), just reached here through sigwait() instead, which IS implemented but backed by the same non-queued pending set. A real fix needs a genuine per-signal queue (count or list, not a bit) threaded through raise()/sigwait()/sigpending() alike, out of scope here
